# Yeti Family 42-Form Tai Chi 70s Panel Images and 3x2 Grids

Image prompts for `prompts/yeti-family-42-form-tai-chi-70s-storyboard.md`, which holds the video prompts. Each 10-second video has, under its own heading, six panel image prompts, one per beat of its storyboard table, timed and framed by the panel map in section 6 of `.agents/rules/cinematic-direction.md`. Each draws its panel's picture as a full single image with the full character lock of each Yeti in it from `.agents/rules/character-consistency.md`, the shot's light, and the panel's faces in its `Expressions:` sentence, as sections 2 to 4 of `.agents/rules/prompt-assembly.md` set out. Each video's 3x2 grid is composed from its six panel images by `compose_grid.ps1`; it has no prompt and is never drawn by an image model, so the grid and the panel images are the same six pictures.

42 panel images and seven grids in total, all in `3x2-timed-storyboard-images/yeti-family-42-form-tai-chi/`: each panel image as `Panel_<P>_V<N>.jpg` and each grid as `Grid_V<N>.jpg`, where `V` is the video number. The panel images are vertical 9:16, and each grid is a 1920x1080 horizontal canvas.

To make a video's images:

1. Generate the six panel images one prompt at a time, each with the family reference image attached as a character reference, the prompt pasted whole, and the output set to the film's aspect ratio. Save each under the name its `Image:` line gives.
2. Check every panel image against its prompt and against the look check in section 7 of `.agents/rules/prompt-assembly.md`, which starts from the Final Consistency Checklist in `.agents/rules/character-consistency.md`: each Yeti the same individual as in every other panel, in its own colour with its own tuft, Babu Yeti about half as tall as a grown-up beside him, nothing worn and nothing on any head, no extra Yeti, the face the prompt names, the framing, angle, and light the prompt names, every listed character and animal present in the stated number and no one else, and no text. Then check the six side by side for the same look across the set. Regenerate any image that fails; a wrong image is cheaper to redo than a wrong clip.
3. Film-specific checks. All 42 Tai Chi forms are performed empty-handed with soft rounded paws—no swords, staffs, fans, or weapons of any kind. No glowing energy blasts, aura waves, or force fields appear. All three Yetis are present in every panel, arranged strictly with Papa Yeti on the left, Babu Yeti in the center, and Mama Yeti on the right. Paws and arms remain at or below chest/shoulder height, never raised directly overhead, preserving traditional martial arts structure and character proportions.
4. Compose the grid from the six saved panel images: `pwsh -File .agents/skills/create-3x2-timed-image/compose_grid.ps1 -File prompts/yeti-family-42-form-tai-chi-70s-3x2.md -Video <N>`. Compose it again whenever a panel image changes.
5. If the image model refuses a prompt, the filter causes in the storyboard's agent instructions apply to it too. Change only that prompt's picture and effects text and keep every lock, character locks included.

To use them in Google Flow, set the output format to the film's aspect ratio, generate the video's clip in ingredients-to-video mode with the video's `Character locks:` block and table from the storyboard pasted as the prompt, and attach one of these:

- Panel route: the six panel images and the family reference image. That is seven images, the most Flow accepts for one clip, and it gives Flow every beat of the clip at full size.
- Grid route: the composed grid and the family reference image.

Never attach more than seven images, and never set a panel image or a grid as a start or an end frame. Check the clip against the six panel images, each at its timecode.

## Video 1 - The Awakening Forms

Film position: 00:00 - 00:10.

Stage: Exposition (Forms 1–6).

Video prompt: the Video 1 `Character locks:` block and table in `prompts/yeti-family-42-form-tai-chi-70s-storyboard.md`.

Grid image: `3x2-timed-storyboard-images/yeti-family-42-form-tai-chi/Grid_V1.jpg`, composed from the six panel images below by `compose_grid.ps1`.

Environment: A tranquil mountain terrace with ancient stone flags and mossy stone steps, flanked by snow-dusted Himalayan pine trees with misty distant peaks under an overcast dawn sky. Lighting: Soft, diffused morning daylight filtering through light mountain mist, with cool blue ambient fill from the distant peaks and gentle warm rim light highlighting the fur, low contrast, quiet and serene. On the left, Papa Yeti: a stylized, friendly 3D animated grown-up father Yeti rendered in high-quality 3D CGI with ultra-realistic, soft, individually stranded fur. He is entirely covered in thick, fluffy, vibrant sapphire-blue fur, one solid sapphire-blue colour from head to toe, with a smooth, slightly matte light-blue face and light-blue paws that blend naturally into the fur. He has a large, solid, broad-shouldered silhouette, a generous round fluffy belly, long heavy arms, thick sturdy legs, and oversized rounded feet; he is the largest Yeti in the family, about twice the height of the baby Yeti. His proportionally large, smooth, rounded, fully furred head has a wide, soft face with full rounded cheeks and a broad light-blue muzzle, large glossy cartoon eyes with dark pupils, blue-grey irises, and clean white sclera, dark thick soft-edged gently arched eyebrows, and a small round nose slightly darker than his face. When his mouth is open it shows clean, square, rounded white teeth. His fur is short and tidy on the forehead and face, and thick and full around the cheeks, jaw, chest, shoulders, belly, forearms, and legs, forming a fluffy beard-like fullness around the lower cheeks and jaw in the same sapphire blue. On top of his head is a playful tuft of messy sapphire-blue fur made of several soft, curved, unruly locks sweeping upward and slightly outward; the tuft is the only thing on his head. His paws have soft, rounded, curled fingers and toes with no claws. He wears nothing but his own fur.
